Document 005: 10月企業物価指数 ～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～

Economic Indicators

2026 年 11 月 12 日

1/2 
10 月企業物価指数 
～前年比＋7.1％、電力や石油・石炭製品を中心に幾分加速～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  阿原 健一郎 
（℡：050-5470-4868）

1. 国内企業物価指数は幾分加速 
 本日公表された 10 月の国内企業物価指数は前年比＋7.1％と、前月（同＋6.8％）から 
伸びが幾分加速した。前年比寄与度で内訳を見ると、玄米や豚肉などの「農林水産物」のマイナ
ス寄与が前月から拡大した一方、「電力・都市ガス・水道」や「石油・石炭製品」、「化学製品」の
プラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表１）。 
 背景には、原油やナフサの価格上昇が石油製品や化学製品へ波及していることに加え、電力に
おいても、過去の輸入燃料価格の上昇がタイムラグを伴って燃料費調整単価に反映され、全体を
押し上げていることがある。農林水産物の価格下落が一部で下押し要因となっているものの、エ
ネルギー関連を中心としたコスト上昇圧力は依然として根強い。

２. 輸入物価指数はわずかに加速 
 10 月の輸入物価指数は前年比＋24.8％と、前月（同＋23.8％）から伸びがわずかに加速した。
「石油・石炭・天然ガス」のプラス寄与が前月から拡大し、ヘッドラインを押し上げた（図表
２）。 
 為替変動の影響を除いた契約通貨ベースでも同＋19.2％と、前月（同＋17.3％）から伸びが拡大
しており、海外の資源価格上昇が輸入物価を押し上げている。輸入コストの増加が時間差を伴っ
て国内の製品価格に波及することを踏まえると、先行きも価格転嫁を通じて消費者物価への上昇
圧力が残る可能性がある。

以 上

なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

115 年 10 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
10 月出口 967.6 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 10.9%，較上
年同月增 58.9%(按新臺幣計算增 66.6%)；累 計 1 至 10 月出口 7,582.9
億美元，較上年同期增 47.7%(按新臺幣計算增 51.1%)。 
（二）進口 
10 月進口 688.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 8.3%，較上
年同月增 75.1%(按新臺幣計算增 83.5%)；累 計 1 至 10 月進口 5,697.6
億美元，較上年同期增 44.6%(按新臺幣計算增 47.6%)。 
（三）出入超 
10 月出超 278.8 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 63.3 億美元；
累計 1 至 10 月出超 1,885.4 億美元，較上年同期增 689.7 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續拓展，
加上適逢傳統外銷旺季，以及價格攀升效應，10 月出口較上年同月
增 358.7 億美元(+58.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 476.0 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增
200.7 億美元(+72.9%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增 151.6 億美元
(+67.5%)、電腦之零附件增 18.5 億美元(+1.0 倍)、儲存媒體增 17.3
億美元(+2.1 倍)、交換器及路由器增 11.7 億美元(+1.3 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 326.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 124.3
億美元(+61.4%)，其中積體電路增 120.5 億美元(+63.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.9 億美元(+38.5%)、電容器及電阻器增 1.2 億美元(+48.6%)。 
3. 基本金屬及其製品出口 28.8 億美元，較上年同月增 7.6 億美元
(+35.7%)，其中銅及其製品增 4.7 億美元(+1.2 倍)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.9 億美元(+16.4%)。 
4. 機械出口 28.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 7.5 億美元
(+35.5%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.9 億美元(+57.5%)、機器、
工廠或實驗室設備增 1.4 億美元(+70.0%)。 
5. 礦產品出口 15.5 億美元，較上年同月增 6.5 億美元(+71.6%)，其
中石油煉製品增 5.7 億美元(+72.2%)。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路、資通產品
及半導體設備進口持續強勁，加上國際原物料行情居高，10 月總進
口較上年同月增 295.5 億美元(+75.1%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口 458.3 億美元，較上年同月增 195.0 億美元(+74.0%)，
其中電子零組件增 136.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)、礦產品增 22.8 億美元
(+58.0%)、電腦之零附件增 16.2 億美元(+70.0%)、基本金屬增 6.2
億美元(+33.4%)、塑化原料增 5.0 億美元(+22.0%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 163.4 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通設備增 46.9 億美元(+2.0 倍)、半導體設備增 22.4 億美元
(+83.4%)、交通運輸設備增 7.6 億美元(+7.0 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 61.1 億美元，較上年同月增 18.8 億美元(+44.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.0 億美元(+5.3 倍)、食品增 0.5 億
美元(+5.1%)、小客車增 0.4 億美元(+10.2%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月對美國、歐洲、日本出口創新高 
1. 對美國出口 329.3 億美元，較上年同月增 118.1 億美元(+55.9%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 98.3 億美元(+56.6%)、電子零組件增 13.5
億美元(+1.7 倍)、機械增 2.4 億美元(+41.6%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 204.4 億美元，較上年同月增 61.4 億美元
(+42.9%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+48.0%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.6 億美元(+36.5%)、基本金屬及其製品增 3.2 億美元
(+95.2%)。 
3. 對東協出口 184.6 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+83.8%)，
其中電子零組件增 38.6 億美元(+75.5%)、資通與視聽產品增 36.5
億美元(+1.4 倍)、礦產品增 5.0 億美元(+2.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 59.5 億美元，較上年同月增 18.7 億美元(+46.0%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 10.7 億美元(+47.9%)、電子零組件增 3.3 億
美元(+81.3%)、機械增 1.2 億美元(+50.8%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.7 億美元，較上年同月增 12.2 億美元(+49.6%)，其
中電子零組件增 7.5 億美元(+63.2%)、資通與視聽產品增 3.2 億美
元(+1.3 倍)、化學品增 0.5 億美元(+47.0%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月對美國出口 2,407.1 億美元，較上年同期增 891.1 億美
元(+58.8%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,805.3 億美元，增 416.6 億美
元(+30.0%)，對東協出口 1,524.1 億美元，增 547.6 億美元(+56.1%)，
對歐洲出口 481.3 億美元，增 157.1 億美元(+48.5%)，對日本出口 314.4
億美元，增 70.5 億美元(+28.9%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較

美國增 23.0%、自歐洲增 12.1%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-9 月主要國家/地區出口均呈上升，其中南韓(1-10 月)、香港、
新加坡受 AI 相關需求帶動科技供應鏈及區域貿易活動熱絡，累計出
口分別年增 60.6%、52.3%、41.3%；中國大陸、美國各年增 21.3%
及 14.3%，德國(1-8 月)、日本各增 8.8%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步成長，累計 1-9 月以香港進口年增 51.8%較
高，新加坡、中國大陸、南 韓 (1-10 月)分別年增 39.0%、27.5%及 21.4%，
德國(1-8 月)、日本、美國各增 8.4%、8.2%、6.7%。

七、綜合分析
（一）隨全球人工智慧(AI)基礎建設投資強勁成長，帶動相關供應鏈需求擴
增，加以外銷產品價格攀升，10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，首度突破
900 億美元，年增 58.9%，連續 36 個月正成長；進口 688.9 億美元，
同為歷年單月最高，年增 75.1%，主要受惠於 AI 產業之國際分工與
出口引申需求增加，以及國際原物料行情高漲；出超 278.8 億美元亦
創新高。累計 1 至 10 月出口年增 47.7%、進口增 44.6%。
（二）10 月出口主要貨品中，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件出口值雙創單月
新高，各年增 72.9%及 61.4%，主因 AI、高效能運算及雲端服務等應
用商機熱絡；其餘貨類出口平均增 25.7%，其中礦產品因油價支撐增
71.6%，機械、電機產品受惠於半導體業者擴充產能及 AI 需求外溢，
增幅皆達 3 成，基本金屬及其製品亦增 35.7%。累 計 1 至 10 月資通與
視聽產品、電子零組件合占總出口比重約 8 成，年增 60.6%。

（三）10 月對主要市場出口年增率均逾 4 成，以對東協增 83.8%較突出，
對美國、日本、歐洲出口規模皆為單月新高，各增 55.9%、49.6%及
46.0%，對中國大陸與香港增 42.9%。累 計 1 至 10 月對五大市場出口
增幅介於 28%~59%，其中對美國出口年增 58.8%、對東協增 56.1%，
占總出口比重各為 31.7%及 20.1%，分別寫下近 36 年同期及歷年同
期新高紀錄。
（四）展望未來，全球經濟仍面臨地緣政治衝突、美國貿易政策走向等不
確定因素，惟隨人工智慧應用逐漸擴散至推論、代理式 AI，且國際
雲端服務業者持續布建 AI 基礎設施，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供
應鏈需求延續，加以科技新品相繼推 出、年底消費旺季來臨之挹注，
我國第 4 季出口可望維持強勁成長態勢。

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026年 11月16日

1/5 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（１次速報値） 
～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅マイナス成長も、景気回復の基調は維持 
本日内閣府から公表された 2026年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率（１次速報）は前期比年率
▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と、４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった1。個人消費が前期比▲0.3％
と減少したほか、住宅投資や公共投資も減少し、成長率を押し下げている。一方、設備投資は前
期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えした。概ね事前の市場予想（前期比年率＋0.2％、
筆者予想：同▲0.4％）の範囲内であり、サプライズはない。 
今回減少した個人消費については、過度に悲観する必要はないだろう。４-６月期にみられた自
動車やエアコンなどの耐久財需要の一時的な押し上げが一巡したことに加え、７-９月期には台風
や大雨、長雨などの天候不順が相次いだことも下押し要因となったとみられる。一方、名目賃金
の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、家計の所得環境は足元で改善している。食料品
や日用品などを中心とした物価の高止まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、今回の減
少をもって個人消費の基調が悪化したと判断する必要はないだろう。 
一方、設備投資が高い伸びとなったことは好材料だ。今回の増加には前期の特殊要因の反動も
含まれているが、それを除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持す
るなか、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。企業
部門の底堅さが改めて確認されたと評価できる。 
このように、７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰは４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となったが、減少幅は僅か
だ。前期まで３四半期連続で比較的高い成長が続いていたことに加え、今回の個人消費の減少に
は、耐久財需要の剥落や８～９月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因が影響したとみられる
点も重要だ。今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろ
う。家計部門は力強さを欠く一方、設備投資を中心に企業部門は堅調さを維持している。家計と
企業の回復に濃淡はみられるものの、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると評価で
きる。

1 ＧＤＰ統計では、季節調整のかけ直しや基礎統計の改定に伴い過去の値も毎回改定される。25年４-６月期が前期比
年率＋0.8％（改定前＋0.5％）、７-９月期が▲1.5％（同▲1.5％）、10-12月期が＋1.1％（同＋1.1％）、26年１-３月期
が＋1.9％（同＋1.9％）、４-６月期が＋1.7％（同＋1.4％）などとなった。符号の変化等はみられず、景気認識に変更
を迫るほどではなかった。

### Page 2

2/5

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
今回のＧＤＰを評価する際に注意が必要なのは、輸入と公的在庫の動きである。 
４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を通
じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油が
放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt（前
期比年率寄与度▲2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げは、
公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じた。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって原油輸入が
回復したため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因となった。一方、輸入の回復に伴って国
家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小した。この
結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.5％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大き
く押し上げている。 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げ（前期比年率寄与度▲2.2％Pt）と公的在庫変動による押し上げ
（同寄与度＋2.0％Pt）は、ともに原油の供給環境が改善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別
に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が回避されたことである。ＧＤ
Ｐ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用可能
な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むしろ前
向きに捉えるべきだろう。

10-12月期はプラス成長を予想 
 先行きについても、景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持すると予想する。７-９月期は４四半期ぶり
のマイナス成長となったが、耐久財特需の剥落や天候不順による一時的な下押しが和らぐことか
ら、10-12月期には再びプラス成長に戻る可能性が高い。名目賃金の高い伸びや雇用の改善が続く
なか、所得環境の改善が個人消費を下支えするとみられる。 
ただし、今後は物価上昇率の再加速が個人消費の重荷になると予想される。既往の原油高や原
材料費、物流費などの上昇が時間差を伴って川下へ波及しつつある。今後は食料品や日用品など
への価格転嫁がさらに進むことに加え、電気・ガス代も上昇するだろう。物価上昇率の加速によ
り、足元で続いている実質賃金の改善が一服する恐れがある。個人消費は先行き持ち直すとみる
が、回復ペースは力強さを欠くとみられる。 
一方、企業部門については今後も底堅い推移が期待できる。企業収益が高水準を維持するな
か、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。輸入資本
財価格や建設コストの上昇は下押し要因となるものの、設備投資は緩やかな増加基調を続けると
予想する。また、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さやグローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大も、輸

### Page 3

3/5 
出や生産を下支えするだろう。 
このように、10-12月期は個人消費が緩やかに持ち直すことに加え、設備投資や輸出の増加が景
気を下支えし、再びプラス成長になると予想する。物価高による個人消費への下押しには注意が
必要だが、企業部門の堅調さが維持されることで、日本経済は緩やかな回復を続けるだろう。

需要項目別の詳細 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と２四半期連続で減少した。形態別では、耐久財消費が前期比▲
3.5％（４-６月期：同＋3.6％）、非耐久財消費が同▲0.2％（４-６月期：同▲1.9％）、半耐久財
消費が同▲1.4％（４-６月期：同＋0.4％）、サービス消費が同＋0.1％（４-６月期：同▲0.1％）
となっている。４-６月期には環境性能割の廃止を受けた自動車販売の増加や、27年４月の省エネ
基準強化を控えたエアコンの前倒し購入など、耐久財消費の特需がみられたが、７-９月期にはこ
うした押し上げ効果が一巡した。また、８～９月には記録的な豪雨や台風、長雨などの天候不順
が相次いだことが、外出手控えによる消費機会の減少や旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて下押し
に繋がったとみられる。また、非耐久財消費にも弱さがみられる。食料品や日用品の価格上昇が
続くなか、家計の節約志向が根強いことに加え、天候不順による季節商品の販売減少なども下押
しに効いた可能性がある。 
もっとも、名目賃金の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、所得環境は足元で改善し
ており、家計を取り巻く環境が悪化しているわけではない。食料品を中心とした価格水準の高止
まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、４-６月期には高校授業料の実質無償化拡充や学
校給食費無償化に伴う個人消費から政府消費への振り替わりなどの特殊要因があり、今回７-９月
期についても耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが大きく影響したとみられ
る。個人消費が力強さを欠いていることは確かだが、今回の減少をもって消費の基調が悪化した
と判断する必要はないだろう。 
先行きについては、耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが和らぐことに加
え、賃金上昇や雇用の改善が下支えとなることから、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直すと予想する。
ただし、26年度後半には既往の輸入コスト上昇の価格転嫁が進み、食料品や日用品などの値上が
りや電気・ガス代の上昇が家計の実質購買力を圧迫する可能性が高い。物価上昇率の再加速によ
って実質賃金の改善が一服するリスクがあり、消費の回復ペースは当面緩やかなものにとどまる
だろう。 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、３四半期ぶりに増加した。４-６月期には特許権
の海外譲渡に伴う研究開発投資の減少という特殊要因により押し下げられていたが、７-９月期に
はその影響が剥落したことが前期比での押し上げ要因となった。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を
除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応
した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は引き続き強い。各種調査でも企業の設
備投資計画は高い伸びを維持しており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断
できる。

### Page 4

4/5
先行きについても、輸入資本財価格や建設コストの上昇が設備投資の下押し要因となるもの
の、高水準の企業収益や根強い省力化・デジタル・ＡＩ関連投資需要が下支えとなり、緩やかな
増加基調が続くと予想する。
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れている。
両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減となった。４-６月期には大口要因によって研究開発サービスの
受取が急増していたが、７-９月期にはその反動によってサービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（４-６月
期：＋8.9％）と大幅に減少した。一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（４-６月期：▲0.8％）と持ち
直している。グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、半導体製造装置などの資本財輸出が好
調だったほか、４-６月期に大きく落ち込んだ中東向け輸出に回復の動きがみられたことも財輸出

の押し上げ要因になった。サービス輸出の特殊要因による振れを除けば、海外景気の底堅さとＡ
Ｉ関連需要の拡大を背景に、輸出は緩やかな増加基調を維持していると評価できる。
先行きについても、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さに加え、グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の
拡大が半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出を下支えすることから、緩やかな増加基調が続くと
予想する。
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と増加した。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が減少していた
が、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％Pt（前期比年率寄与度▲2.4％Pt）と大幅なマイナスになった。
ただし、輸入増加による成長率の押し下げは、原油などの供給環境が改善したことに伴うもの
であり、需要の弱さを示すものではない。また、輸入増加の裏側では、国家備蓄原油の取り崩し
が一服したことに伴って公的在庫変動が前期比で＋0.5％ポイント、前期比年率では＋2.0％ポイ
ントの大幅プラス寄与となっている。両者は一体として評価する必要があるだろう。

民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.0％Ptと、成長率にはほぼニュートラルだった。製品在庫と原
材料在庫が押し上げに寄与した一方、流通在庫と仕掛品在庫が下押し要因になった。
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と、２四半期連続で減少した。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因となっ
ており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。先行きについても、建設コストの高止まり
や金利上昇の影響から、低調な推移が続く可能性が高い。
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と概ね横ばいとなった。４-６月期に前期比＋1.5％と高い伸びとなっ
た後、増勢が一服している。もっとも、医療・介護関連の支出などは趨勢的に増加しており、政
府消費の弱さは前期の反動による一時的なものと考えられる。
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と減少した。政府の国土強靱化関連工事などが下支えにはなっている
が、建設コストの上昇が実質公共投資の下押し要因になっているとみられる。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

Indicators Update 
2026年 9月機械受注 
製造業が堅調で7-9月期の民需（船電除く）は4四半期連続で増加

経済調査部 エコノミスト 吉井 希祐

[要約] 
◼ 2026年9月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比▲0.3％と2カ月ぶりに減少し、コン
センサス（Bloomberg調査：同＋1.5％）を下回った。民需（船電除く）を 3カ月移動
平均で見ると同＋0.3％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみ
られる」に据え置いた。 
◼ 製造業からの受注額は前月比＋3.9％と 2カ月連続で増加した。化学工業では大型案件
が 1件あり、全体を押し上げた。非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額は同＋1.1％と 2
カ月連続で増加した。運輸業・郵便業や情報サービス業では大型案件が 1 件ずつあり、
全体を大きく押し上げた。 
◼ 先行きの民需（船電除く）は緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。企業の設備投資意
欲は強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待され
よう。ただし、中東情勢が悪化すれば、事業環境の不確実性の高まりやエネルギー・
原材料価格の一段の高騰への懸念から、企業の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れが
ある。

【総括】船電除く民需は小幅に減少したものの、製造業・非製造業はいずれも増加 
2026年 9月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比▲0.3％と 2カ月ぶりに減少し、コンセン
サス（Bloomberg調査：同＋1.5％）を下回った（図表2）。製造業は2カ月連続で増加し、非製
造業（船電除く）も 2カ月連続で増加した 0F
1（図表 3）。民需（船電除く）を 3カ月移動平均で
見ると同＋0.3％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみられる」に据
え置いた。 
2026年7-9月期の民需（船電除く）は前期比＋1.2％と4四半期連続で増加したものの、6月
末時点の企業見通し（同＋4.9％）には届かなかった。製造業は同＋8.0％と 2四半期ぶりに増
加した。業種別で見ると、電気機械（同＋31.4％）が全体を大きく押し上げた。他方、非製造
業（船電除く）は同▲0.8％と2四半期連続で減少した。 
図表 2：機械受注額と名目設備投資

（注）季節調整値。太線は 3カ月移動平均。

【製造業】大型案件があった化学機械を中心に増加 
2026年 9月の製造業からの受注額は前月比＋3.9％と 2カ月連続で増加した。機種別に見る
と、産業機械、電子・通信機械、工作機械、輸送機械が増加した一方、原動機・重電機は減少
した（図表 4左、大和総研による季節調整値）。業種別では、17業種中 10業種が増加した。化
学工業（同＋57.0％）では化学機械の大型案件が 1 件あり、全体を押し上げた。業務用機械
（同＋80.5％）、その他製造業（同＋24.6％）などからの受注も増加した。他方、はん用・生産
用機械（同▲14.8％）や非鉄金属（同▲49.0％）などからの受注は減少した。

【非製造業】大型案件があった運輸業・郵便業や情報サービス業が大きく押し上げる 
2026年9月の非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額は前月比＋1.1％と2カ月連続で増加した。
機種別に見ると、輸送機械、電子・通信機械、工作機械が増加した一方、産業機械、原動機・
重電機は減少した（図表 4右、大和総研による季節調整値）。業種別では、11業種中 3業種が
増加した。運輸業・郵便業（同＋51.3％）では鉄道車両、情報サービス業（同＋25.0％）では
通信機の大型案件が 1件ずつあり、全体を大きく押し上げた。他方、リース業（同▲49.4％）
や農林漁業（同▲16.5％）をはじめ、幅広い業種からの受注が減少した。

図表 4：業種別・機種別に見た機械受注額の動き

除外したが、26年9月は前月比＋10.3％であった。

【外需】9件の大型案件が下支えし、3カ月ぶりに増加 
2026年 9月の外需は前月比＋6.2％と 3カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 5）。機種別では、電子・
通信機械、原動機・重電機、産業機械、工作機械が増加した一方、輸送機械は減少した（図表
6、大和総研による季節調整値）。電子・通信機械で 3件、原動機・重電機で 2件、輸送機械で
4件の合計9件の大型案件があった。 
機械受注の外需動向を地域別に見る上で参考になる工作機械受注を確認すると、9月の外需
は前月比＋2.6％と4カ月連続で増加した（日本工作機械工業会、大和総研による季節調整値、
図表 7）。 アジア（中国を除く、同＋13.9％）からの受注が好調で 1F
2、全体を押し上げた。他方、
欧州（EU＋英国、同▲15.2％）と米国（同▲3.0％）からの受注は 4 カ月ぶりに減少し、中国
（同▲0.6％）からの受注は 2カ月ぶりに減少した（図表8）。  
工作機械受注は 10月分がすでに公表されており、内需は前月比＋2.2％と 2カ月ぶりに増加
した一方、外需は同▲5.8％と5カ月ぶりに減少した。

【先行き】民需（船電除く）の緩やかな増加基調を見込むも中東情勢等が下振れリスク 
先行きの民需（船電除く）は、緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。日銀短観の 9月調査に
おける「生産・営業用設備判断 DI」（先行き、全規模）を見ると、製造業（▲6％pt）と非製造
業（▲7％pt）のいずれでも、設備の不足感が示されている。企業の設備投資意欲は強く、人
手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI関連投資などが引き続き期待されよう。 
内閣府が公表した 2026年 10-12月期の見通しによれば、民需（船電除く）は前期比＋4.3％
と 5四半期連続での増加が見込まれており、企業の設備投資意欲は引き続き強いとみられる。
製造業は同＋2.6％、非製造業（船電除く）は同＋5.2％と、いずれも堅調に推移する見込みだ。 
AI関連投資に関しては、米国のハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる企業群の巨額の設備投資など
を背景に、関連製品を生産・輸出するはん用・生産用機械 2F
3や電気機械、情報通信機械などか
らの受注が増加傾向にあり、今後もこれらの業種からの受注が民需全体を押し上げるとみられ
る（図表 9）。ただし、米国では一部の AI関連企業の収益性や財務面への懸念に加え、中間選
挙の結果を受け、AIやデータセンターに対する規制強化への懸念が強まっている。こうした懸
念が現実化し、AI関連需要をけん引するハイパースケーラーの設備投資の増勢が大きく鈍化す
れば、関連する日本企業の設備投資が下振れする可能性があり、注意が必要だ 3F
4。 
中東情勢をめぐっては、ホルムズ海峡の事実上の封鎖が続くなど、依然として不透明感の強
い状況にある。情勢が悪化すれば、事業環境の不確実性の高まりやエネルギー・原材料価格の
一段の高騰への懸念から、企業の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れがある 4F
5。 
図表 9：業種別の製造業の機械受注額への寄与度

（注）製造業の受注額（季節調整値の 3カ月移動平均）の2025年1月からの変化率に対する累積寄与度。 
（出所）内閣府統計より大和総研作成

3 半導体・フラットパネルディスプレイ製造装置製造業などが含まれる。 
4 米国のハイパースケーラーの設備投資動向が世界経済に与える影響については、畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイ
パースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポート、2026年11月 12日）を参照。 
5 米国の中間選挙後の原油価格高騰リスクについては、神田慶司・畑中宏仁「米中間選挙による日本経済への
影響」（大和総研レポート、2026年 11月17日）を参照。

Document 095: 2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026年11月 17日

1/3 
2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し 
～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 2027年の春闘賃上げ率を、厚生労働省ベースで 5.15％（2026年：5.18％）、連合ベースで
4.98％（同 5.01％）と予測する。2027年春闘でもほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、厚生労
働省ベースでは４年連続で５％台が実現するだろう。連合は 2027 年春闘の要求目安を前年
よりやや引き上げ、経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。ＵＡゼンセ
ンも前年並みの高い要求水準を示すなど、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
○ 構造的な人手不足が続くなか、人材の確保・定着に向けた賃上げの必要性は高い。企業収益
も総じて堅調であり、高水準の利益が賃上げ余力を支えている。2026年の物価上昇率が前年
から鈍化することは賃上げ率の下押し要因となる。一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初に
は物価上昇率が再び高まる可能性があり、実質賃金の安定的な改善を求める労使双方の問題
意識も強い。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続
する環境は維持されている。 
○ ４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、日本銀行にとっても金融政策の正常化に向けて前
向きな材料となる。特に、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃
金を設定するようになっているかどうかが、２％の物価安定目標の定着を見極めるうえで重
要となる。

2027年春闘でも前年並みの高い賃上げ率を予想 
2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 5.15％と予測する（厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結
状況」ベース）。2024年春闘では5.33％、2025年では 5.52％、2026年でも 5.18％と３年連続で
５％台の高い賃上げが実現したが、2027年もほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、４年連続で５％
台となるだろう。 
春闘においては、厚生労働省よりも公表時期が早い、連合による集計値が注目されることが多
い。この連合ベースの数字では、2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 4.98％と予測する（2026年春闘：
5.01％）。５％台に乗るかどうかは微妙なところだが、こちらも歴史的にみれば高い賃上げとなる

### Page 2

2/3 
だろう。

組合側、経営側ともトーンダウンはみられず  
組合側、経営側とも賃上げに前向きな姿勢を維持している。連合は 10月22日に公表した2027
年春闘基本構想において、定昇込みの賃上げ要求を「６％中心、５％以上」、ベースアップ等の賃
上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、前年の賃上げ「５％以上」、ベア「３％以上」よりも要求
姿勢をやや強めた。経営側も賃上げの継続に前向きだ。経団連の経営労働政策委員会報告原案で
は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられていると報じられた。また、筒井経団連会長
は 11月９日の記者会見で、ベア実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードとする方針を堅持する考え
を示した。また、主要産業別労働組合組織では、ＵＡゼンセンが定昇込み６％、ベア等４％とい
う前年と同等の高い要求方針素案を示している。中東情勢悪化によるコスト上昇という逆風があ
るなかでも、労使ともに賃上げ姿勢がトーンダウンしている様子は窺えない。もっとも、連合の
要求目安は強まったものの、ＵＡゼンセンの要求率は前年と同水準であり、現時点では賃上げ率
のさらなる加速を見込むほどの材料が揃っているわけではないことには注意が必要だ。

物価は前年から鈍化も、人手不足と好調な企業収益が押し上げ要因に 
高い賃上げを支える最大の要因は、構造的な人手不足である。生産年齢人口の減少が続くな
か、企業の人手不足感は依然として強く、人材の確保・定着は重要な経営課題となっている。特
に若年層を中心に採用競争が厳しくなっており、既存人材のつなぎ止めのためにも企業は継続的
な賃上げを行う必要がある。ここ数年の春闘では、こうした人手不足を背景に高い賃上げが実現
しており、企業の賃金設定行動にも変化が生じている。2027年春闘でも人手不足を背景とした賃
上げ圧力は強いと考えられる。 
好調な企業収益も、高い賃上げを支える材料になる。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇は企業収
益の下押し要因となっているものの、価格転嫁の進展やＡＩ関連需要の拡大などを背景に、企業
収益は総じて堅調に推移しており、水準も非常に高い。企業規模や業種によって差はあるもの
の、企業収益が全体として高水準にあり、賃上げ余力は十分に確保されていると見てよい。 
一方、物価については、2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化することが賃上げ率の下押し要因
になるとみられる。春闘交渉では前年の暦年の物価上昇率が参照されることが多いが、2026年の
上昇率（ＣＰＩ総合）は前年比＋２％前後と、2025年の前年比＋3.2％から鈍化する見込みであ
り、物価高への対応という観点からの賃上げ圧力は弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、暦年でみれば物価上昇率は鈍化する一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初には再び
高まり、ＣＰＩは前年比＋３％台に達する可能性がある。この場合、実質賃金が再びマイナスに
転じる恐れもある。そもそも、過去数年にわたって実質賃金が減少してきたことで、家計には賃
上げの恩恵を十分に実感できないという不満が残っている。実質賃金の改善の遅れへの問題意識
は組合側、経営側にも共有されており、実質賃金を安定的に増加させることは 2027年春闘でも重
要な課題となるだろう。春闘交渉の時点で物価上昇率の再加速が意識されれば、春闘賃上げ率の

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
上振れ要因となる可能性もあることには注意したい。

２％「定着」判断の重要な材料に  
以上を踏まえ、2027年春闘では5.15％と、ほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げを予想する（厚生労働省
ベース）。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続する環境
は維持されていると考えられる。 
４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、賃金上昇の持続性を重視する日本銀行にとっても前
向きな材料となるだろう。特に重要なのは、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを
前提として賃金を設定するようになっているかどうかである。2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍
化するなかでも高い賃上げが維持されれば、こうした賃金設定が広がりつつあることを示す一つ
の材料となる。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定目標とおおむね整合的な水準に達し
たとの認識を示したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判断していない。2027年春
闘は、その定着を見極めるうえでも重要な判断材料となるだろう。

みられず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所 
2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確

Document 100: 2317鴻海 宏遠

### Page 1

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

1 
2317 鴻海精密工業(FOXCONN)

報告日期：2026/11/13 研究員：曾智楷(Kevin Tseng) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆，YoY +46%，毛利率 6.02%，EPS 5.51
元。單季營收、營業利益、稅後淨利均創下歷年單季新高。 
 受惠於 AI 營運持續成長及 ICT 產品進入傳統出貨旺季，公司
預期 4Q26 整體營收季對季與年對年均將呈現強勁成長。AI 伺
服器部分，由於新一代機櫃市佔率較上一代提升，預期第四季
AI 機櫃出貨量將維持高雙位數季增。 
 訂單能見度轉佳，公司上修 2026 年全年四大產品展望，對 2027
年全年營運持審慎樂觀看法，預計整體營收維持雙位數成長，
AI 機櫃則預估明年出貨維持高雙位數成長。

一. 3Q26 營收、獲利續創單季新高，EPS 5.51 元 
鴻海 3Q26 營收 3.02 兆，QoQ +19%，YoY +46%，續創歷史新
高，主要受惠於 AI 伺服器呈現強勁成長，其中，第三季 ASIC AI
伺服器出貨量年增 1.5 倍，高於 GPU 約 70%的成長率，帶動整體
AI 機櫃達成高雙位數成長目標，且新一代 AI 機櫃已開始出貨。就
各產品別來看，3Q26 雲端網路產品佔整體營收的 54%；消費智
能產品佔 29%；電腦終端產品佔 12%；元件及其他產品佔 5%，
而獲利方面，3Q26 營業毛利 1815.1 億，QoQ +17%，YoY +39%，
毛利率 6.02%，QoQ -0.1ppts，YoY -0.33ppts，營業利益 1153.5
億，QoQ +22%，YoY+64%，營利率 3.83%，QoQ +0.07ppts，
YoY +0.4ppts，3Q26 業外淨利 33.6 億，稅後淨利 775.2 億，QoQ 
+29%，YoY +34%，單季稅後 EPS 5.51 元，創歷年單季新高。

二. AI 機櫃放量、ICT 產品進入旺季，4Q26 營運有望持
續成長 
公司公告 10 月營收 1 兆 3053 億，MoM +12.7%，YoY +45.7%，
今年累計營收 8.97 兆，YoY +40.4%。展望 4Q26，受惠於 AI 營
運持續成長及 ICT 產品進入傳統出貨旺季，公司預期整體營收季
對季與年對年均將呈現強勁成長。其中，AI 伺服器部分，由於新
一代機櫃市佔率較上一代提升，預期第四季 AI 機櫃出貨量將維持
高雙位數季增。而傳統 ICT 產品，消費智能產品在旺季需求與 ASP
提升下，預期將呈現強勁表現；電腦終端產品則受限於記憶體等
零組件供應吃緊影響，預期將略微季減。預估公司 4Q26 營收 3.59
兆，QoQ +18.7%，YoY +37.9%，毛利率 5.8%，營利率 3.5%，
稅後淨利 811 億元，單季稅後 EPS 5.77 元。

三. 預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 19.08/23.59 元 
展望 2026 年，由於目前訂單能見度較 8 月更佳，公司上調 2026
年全年四大產品展望，整體營運維持強勁成長。預估 2026 全年營
收 11.26 兆，YoY +39%，毛利率 6.0%，營利率 3.7%，稅後淨利
2685 億，全年稅後 EPS 19.08 元。展望 2027 年，公司對明年全
年營運持審慎樂觀看法，預計維持雙位數成長，其中 AI 仍為最主
要的成長動能。受惠於全球算力需求與基礎設施投資，全球主要
CSP 明年持續擴大 AI 資本支出，預期公司雲網產品佔比將在未來
3 到 5 年，從目前的五成以上進一步提高至 60%以上。而 AI 機櫃
部分，預期 2027 年 AI 機櫃出貨維持高雙位數成長，且客戶除了
大型 CSP 客戶之外，明年將加入更多 NeoCloud 客戶，市佔率將
進一步提升。此外，隨 AI 推論需求增加帶動 ASIC 伺服器快速成
長，其高度客製化需求亦有利公司發揮零組件垂直整合優勢以支
撐獲利表現。消費智能產品由於主要客戶產品以高階為主，受記
憶體等供應鏈短缺影響相對有限，再加上新產品開發與規格升級
需求，預計 2027 年整體表現相對穩定。而電腦終端產品，由於記
憶體供應緊張與零組件價格上漲，可能推升終端售價並影響消費
者換機意願，對明年 PC 市場看法較為審慎。預估 2027 全年營收
14.9 兆，YoY +33%，毛 利率 5.4%，營利率 3.3%，稅後淨利 3320
億，全年稅後 EPS 23.59 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 330 元 
考量到 CSP 持續上修 2027 年資本支出展望，AI 伺服器維持強勁
需求，隨著新一代 AI 機櫃推出，有望進一步拓展公司在 AI 伺服
器領域市佔率，帶動公司營收、獲利持續成長。目前公司股價交
易於 2027 年 EPS 約 10~11 倍本益比，評價仍有提升空間，給予
投資建議為買進，目標價 330 元(14x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
鴻海精密工業(FOXCONN)為全球最大 eCMMS(電子化-零元件、
模組機光電垂直整合服務)廠，在電子代工服務領域(EMS)排名全
球第一，市占率超過四成。鴻海近年積極投入「電動車、數位健
康、機器人」三大新興產業以及「人工智慧、半導體、新世代通
訊技術」三項新技術領域，以「3+3」結合作為集團重要的長期發
展策略。在 EMS 板塊，鴻海主要競爭對手為和碩聯合科技、廣達
電腦、緯創資通、仁寶電腦、英業達及立訊精密等。

二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

圖二、2024-2027 年九大 CSP 資本支出總額預估(單位:十億美元)

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、
Tier-2 資料中心業者對 NVIDIA GB/VR 等整櫃式 AI server 方案的
採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大
業者 2026 年的資本支出多翻倍提升，反映出大型 CSP 廠持續擴
建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Limited direct impact on Aspeed and Montage in the near term,

but we see potential long-term demand risks.
The US BIS has tightened export controls to restrict Chinese companies' remote
access to advanced computing capacity through third-country data centers, while
adding licensing requirements for advanced chips shipped to key Southeast Asian
markets, including Singapore and Malaysia (link).
Our view: Gary Yu (China Internet Analyst; link) sees this as another step toward
closing loopholes in Al compute access, potentially slowing GPU deployment by

China-linked cloud operators and raising compliance costs for overseas Al
infrastructure investment. New supplies of advanced chipsets within China to
become increasingly limited. For cloud semis, we see limited direct impact on
Aspeed and Montage in the near term but there are potential long-term demand
risks. Aspeed has a high share in GPU and China server platforms. Montage
interface and retimer chips may also be impacted over the long term.
Please also refer to our recent notes: Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside;

Investor pushback on our cloud semis/old memory downgrades.
This report references export controls and/or entities that may be subject to
export control restrictions. Readers are solely responsible for ensuring that their
investment or trade activities are carried out in compliance with applicable laws.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Key Takeaways
We continue to see margin improvement, with October NPM at 23.4%, above
20.1% in 3Q26.
Margin improvement likely driven by combination of better pricing, improving
mix, and higher utilization for ABF and BT substrates.
Stay OW on both Unimicron and NYPCB – but on a 12-month view, we prefer
Unimicron (12.4x CY27e P/E) over NYPCB (13.0 CY27e P/E).

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers

n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity
Nan Ya PCB (8046.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate

value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.3%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 17%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF and BT demand
n Faster-than-expected pickup in AI and 5G demand
n Stronger-than-expected ASP hike
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.

CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall, creating headwinds for ABF substrate demand and pricing
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.

### Page 1

LG에너지솔루션 (373220)

Earnings Review Company Analysis | 이차전지/화학 | 2026. 11. 4  
일회성 보상금 포함된 실적, ‘30년 ESS 수요 대비 배 이상 높은 셀 3사 Capa 우려

AMPC 4,169억원 포함 영업이익 컨센서스 대폭 상회 
동사는 8일 매출액 9조 6,434억원(YoY 59%, QoQ 27%), AMPC 포함한 영업이익 
7,560억원(OPM 7.8%, YoY 26%, QoQ 567%)로 시장 Consensus(공정공시한 8일 
기준 3,264억원)을 대폭 상회하는 어닝 서프라이즈 발표. 회사는 ESS 출하 급증과 고
전압 Mid-Ni EV 파우치 확대, 견조한 EV향 2170-46시리즈 공급을 물량 중심을 성
장하였고, 폴란드 가동률 개선, 수익성이 견조한 원통형 물량 증가, 북미 ESS 생산성 
개선에 따른 고정비 부담 완화 및 고객사의 일회성 물량 보상금 영향으로 설명. 그러나 
일회성 물량 보상금의 구체적 금액, 반복 발생 여부는 제시하지 않아 불확실성 남겨. 
7,560억원의 영업이익에도 불구하고 영업외손실 9,872억원 발생으로 당기순손실 기
록. 이에는 소형 IT파우치 자산손상 반영되어, 가동률 개선 및 감가상각비 감소를 위해 
Capa를 조정하는 배터리 회사들의 대응 전략이 이번 실적에서 다시 확인

‘30년 ESS 수요 전망들 대비 국내사 Capa 배 이상 높아 
동사는 북미 ESS Capa 확대를 통한 탈중국 수요에 적극 대응하겠다는 전략. 파우치 
라인 추가 전환 및 신규 각형 라인 확보를 통해 북미 ESS Capa를 ‘26년말 50+에서 
‘27년 70+로 제시. 최근 타사에서 ‘26년 미국 ESS 수요를 80~100GWh, 혹은 
130+GWh로, ‘30년 두배 이상, 혹은 240+GWh로 수요 전망에 대해 답변한 바 있음. 
동사 전체 Capa 300GWh(‘25년 연말), SK On 179GWh, SDI 140GWh)임을 감안하
면 ‘30년 약 2배 수요를 가정하면 셀 3사 Capa의 32%에 달하는 수요, 만일 3배 수
요 가정하면 48.5% 수준. 북미 외 시장 가성비 중시 기조 및 중국 LFP 장악(‘25년 
97% M/S)으로 진입장벽 높음을 감안하면 미국 수요 중요. 이를 상쇄하기 위해 EV향 
배터리의 안정적인 공급 지속 필요하며, 이를 위해 동사 원통형은 TESLA의 비미국 시
장 판매량이, 파우치는 시장 자체 축소 지속 여부에 유의할 필요 
상대적으로 가장 견조한 출하량, 그러나 고평가 영역  
셀3사중 EV향 배터리 출하량 및 ESS 탑재에서 현재 가장 견조한 판매량을 보이고 있
으나, AMPC와 일회성 보상금이 영업손익의 상당부분을 견인한다는 점에서 수익성 취
약하다는 단점. 또한 ROE가 (-) 영역 임에도 PBR이 3.x내외로 거래되고 있어 고평가 
지적에서 자유롭기 어려워. 당분간 모회사인 LG화학의 PRS 발행에 따른 단기 오버행 
이슈도 관건. PBR 2.3x(‘25년대비 하락하는 ROE 감안, 20% 할인)적용 목표주가 
290,000원 제시, HOLD 유지. 주식 투자 매력 낮은 상황으로 판단 
 Analyst 정경희 
khchung@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 2

Company Report / LG에너지솔루션 / 2026. 11.4 
LS Securities Research 2 
EV향 3분기 data  
이차전지에서 EV향 비중은 여전히 가장 높다. 타입별로 보면 3분기 글로벌 EV향 이차전
지에서 각형의 M/S가 83%로 지속 증가하고 있으며, 동사가 생산하는 원통형과 파우치의 
경우 합산 M/S가 17%로 하락하였다. 현재 Wh 기준 동사 EV향 배터리에서 3분기 파우
치가 55%, 원통형이 45%로 파우치 비중이 10% 원통형을 상회한다. 
그림1 LGES 원통형, 파우치 타입 EV 향 분기별 탑재량 추이 
그림2

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

그림 2 EV 향 폼팩터 M/S: 각형 확장 vs 원통형/파우치 감소  그림 3 동사 EV 배터리 타입 비중

자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터  자료: SNE Research(2026.10), LS증권 리서치센터

SNE data에 따르면 글로벌 원통형 EV향 수요의 대다수가 TESLA에서 발생하고 있으며, 
‘24~’25년 역성장후 ‘26년 판매량 회복을 나타내는 TESLA 호조로 전년대비 LGES의 EV
향 원통형 판매량은 3분기 증가했다. 반면 파우치의 경우 ‘25년 파우치 탑재 EV 판매 호조
를 나타낸 현대기아차그룹 영향으로 YoY 탑재량이 증가했으나, 올해는 북미 OEM인 GM, 
Ford향 판매가 IRA 일몰로 전년대비 부진함에 따라 3분기 전년대비 25% 감소한 것으로 
나타난다.

그림 9 셀 3 사 분기별 EV 향 탑재량 글로벌 M/S 추이: M/S 감소중

자료: SNE 리서치, LS증권 리서치센터

밸류에이션, 수익성 대비 고평가 영역  
동사는 ‘24년 ROE -4.9%, ‘25년 -5.2%에 이어 ‘26년 -4.4%, ‘27년 -3.4%로 (-) 영역이 
지속되는 것으로 예상한다. ‘24년과 ‘25년 순이익은 (+)지만, 해외 합작회사가 많고 이들 이
익을 연결함에 따라 지배주주순이익은 적자를 기록한 것을 보인다. JV 규모가 축소되고 있
어 이러한 순이익과 지배주주 순이익상의 괴리는 좁혀지겠지만, ROE가 (-)영역 지속을 예
상함에 따라 현재 거래 PBR은 고평가 상황으로 볼 수 있다. 물론 이것은 향후 수익 성장
에 대한 시장 기대가 반영된 것으로 볼 수 있지만, 이차전지 6개사 평균 Consensus 대비 
실제 수익이 ‘23년부터 지속 하향되고 있다는 점, ESS대비 여전히 판매비중이 높은 EV에서 
출하량 정체가 일어나고 있다는 점, 미국 ESS에서 여전히 높은 중국 M/S를 감안하면 이러
한 기대 수준에 대해 다시 검토할 필요가 있다. 이에 더해, 모회사 기준에서는 필요한 과정
이지만 동사 주가 측면에서는 LG화학의 PRS 발행도 단기 오버행으로 당분간 주가에 부담
을 줄 수 있다.

그림 10 셀 3 사, 양∙음극재 3 사 추정치 변화율: ‘23 년부터 추정치 지속 하향 조정

자료: DataGuide, LS증권 리서치센터

미국 ESS가 모든 것을 해결할 수 있나? 
‘25년 글로벌 ESS 시장의 97%를 중국배터리사가 공급했다. LFP 각형이 표준화된 시장인 
것이다. 미국 시장만 중국에 대한 관세 허들, 정책 허들로 한국 배터리사에 기회로 작용한
다. 그러나 ‘26년부터 시작된 FEOC와 관세추가 부담에도 불구, 상반기 미국 ESS 시장의 
76%을 (기타사의 국적을 알 수 없기에 제외하더라도) 중국 셀사가 차지했다. 만일 한국 배
터리 유관회사들이 전망하듯 ‘30년 미국 ESS 시장이 160~200GWh 이상, 240+GWh, 혹
은 240~300GWh가 된다고 해도 3사 전체 Capa의 50%가 채 되지 못한다. EV 수요가 뒷
받침되지 않는다면 미국 ESS만으로는 가동률을 80~90% 이상 올리기 어렵다는 의미다. 특
히 동사는 원통형과 파우치를 생산하고 있으나, 원통형은 북미외 지역 TESLA의 판매에, 
파우치는 올해 출하량이 감소하는 것으로 나타나고 있다. 파우치 고객의 각형 채택 비중 
증가를 감안하면 동사 EV향 배터리 출하량 증가를 확신하기는 어렵다.   
긍정적 기대와 동시에 냉정한 점검이 필요한 시점으로 판단한다.

표2 LG 에너지솔루션 3Q26 실적발표 주요 Q&A 
[Q&A]           
Q1-1. 4Q26 실적 전망과 2027 년 큰 방향은? 
  A. 4Q26 ESS 출하량은 북미 수요 대응으로 QoQ 약 40% 증가 예상 
   유럽 고전압 Mid-Ni 파우치 및 원통형 물량도 연말까지 견조한 출하 지속 전망 
   3Q26 가동을 시작한 북미 JV 효과가 더해져 전사 매출은 QoQ 약 10% 성장 가능할 것으로 예상 
   가동률 개선과 사이트 안정적 램프업은 지속 추진하나 3Q26 일회성 요인 소멸의 기저효과 존재 
   4Q26 혼다 JV ESS CAPA 추가 가동 준비에 따른 초기 고정비 부담으로 수익성 일부 둔화 불가피

2027 년 사업계획은 수립 중이며 전방 수요 변수로 구체적 가이던스 제시는 어려운 단계 
    ESS: 북미 확보 수주 기반으로 유의미한 물량 성장 지속 기대 
    소형: 전략 고객향 EV 원통형과 46 시리즈 신규 애플리케이션 판매로 안정적 성장 예상 
    EV 파우치: 중저가 제품 물량과 북미 EV 향 생산품이 더해져 성장 예상 
   전 사업부 매출 성장 기대하나 핵심 제품 경쟁력·기존 CAPA 효율화·가동률 극대화·원가 개선에 집중 
   2027 년 매출 성장률·영업이익률 등 구체적 수치는 제시 X

Q1-2. 북미 PFE 요건 충족을 위한 LFP 공급망 전략과 중국 관련 주력 양극재 공급사 대응은? 
  A. 미국 생산세액공제 확보를 위해 직접재료비 내 PFE 소재 비중 요건을 충족하는 공급망 구축 필요 
   회사 설명상 PFE 소재 허용 비중은 2026 년 40%에서 2027 년 35% 이하로 강화. Non-PFE 비중과 혼동 유의 
   소재별 소싱 전략을 구체화해 준비 중이며 요건 충족은 차질 없이 진행될 것으로 판단 
   재료비 비중이 큰 양극재는 최근 계약한 국내 업체 외 복수 Non-PFE 업체 조달 옵션 확보

음극재·분리막·전해액도 기존 Non-PFE 공급망에 더해 신규 업체 적극 발굴 
   기존 원가 중심 최적화에서 정책·지정학 리스크를 고려한 공급망 안정성 강화로 전략 확대 
   협력사의 원가·기술·물량 대응력을 종합 평가하고 장기적으로 현지 협력사 발굴·현지화 추진 
   질문한 중국 관련 주력 공급사의 개별 지위·조달 비중에 대한 직접적인 상세 답변 X

Q2-1. ESS 사업의 수익성 전망 및 개선 방안은? 
  A. EV→ESS 라인 전환으로 수요에 맞는 생산체계를 구축하고 북미 고객 적기 공급에 주력 
   다수 사이트 동시 램프업에 어려움이 있었고 고정비 절감 노력에도 수익성 개선 속도는 시장 기대보다 느렸음 
   2026 년 말 50GWh 목표 셀 라인 램프업은 마무리 국면. Link CAPA 도 3Q26 에 이어 4Q26 추가 보강 예정 
   CAPA 안정화와 점진적인 고정비 부담 완화 기대 
   확보 CAPA 효율화, 소재 소싱 다변화로 원재료비·물류비 절감에 집중 
   AI 데이터센터 및 SI 프로젝트 비중 확대를 통한 고객 포트폴리오 최적화 추진 
   셀·시스템 고용량화로 원가 경쟁력을 높여 구조적 수익성 개선 추진 
   구체적 ESS 영업이익률 목표나 개선 폭은 제시 X

Q2-2. 북미 ESS 추가 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 빠른 시장 성장 및 Non-PFE 에 우호적인 정책으로 북미 현지 생산 셀 수요 급증 
   현재 계획된 CAPA 기준으로 2028 년까지 물량은 대부분 확정. 그 이후 물량 중심으로 고객 수주 협의 진행 
   기존 전력망·유틸리티 고객, 이들을 통한 AI 데이터센터 BESS 수요, 하이퍼스케일러 BTM 직접 구매 수요 포함 
   예상 수요가 공급 가능 CAPA 를 초과해 전략적으로 중요한 프로젝트 중심으로 미래 잔여 CAPA 운영 계획 
   추가 고객명·신규 계약량 등은 제시하지 않음. 예상 수요와 확정 수주를 구분

Q3-1. 46 시리즈의 구체적인 수주 현황과 향후 모멘텀은? 
  A. 기존 OEM 부터 EV 스타트업까지 이미 440GWh 이상 물량 확보 
   추가로 다수 글로벌 OEM 과 협의 중이며 가까운 시일 내 의미 있는 규모의 수주 성과 기대 
   제품 경쟁력·지역별 생산 역량으로 46 시리즈 선도 입지를 강화하고 고객 포트폴리오 확대 계획 
   440GWh 는 확보 물량이며 연간 판매량이나 생산 CAPA 가 아님. 추가 계약 규모·고객명은 미공개

### Page 7

Company Report / LG에너지솔루션 / 2026. 11.4 
LS Securities Research 7

Q3-2. Tabless 2170 의 경쟁력과 수주·신규 시장 대응 현황은? 
  A. 기존 제품 대비 낮은 발열·저항으로 안전성 및 고출력에 유리해 HEV·BBU·로봇 등 다양한 용도에 적합 
   오창에서 전동공구용 제품은 이미 양산 중  
   BBU 향 LFP 2170 은 2Q27 양산 목표로 준비 중 
   HEV 향은 2027 년 말 애리조나에서 양산 계획  
   LFP 2170 은 NCM 대비 열안전성 테스트에서 우수한 성능을 보여 고객의 긍정적 피드백 확보 
   고객 니즈·수요에 적극 대응해 신시장 경쟁력 확보 추진. 수주 규모 등 정량 정보는 제시하지 않음

Q4-1. 재무 건전성 개선 방안과 2027 년 자금 조달 계획은? 
  A. 선제 CAPA 투자 자금은 저리 정책자금 장기차입 및 원화·외화 회사채로 안정적으로 조달해 왔음 
   주요 투자는 대부분 집행됐고 CAPEX 는 2025 년을 기점으로 유의미하게 감소 중 
   향후 영업현금흐름 확대분을 차입금 상환에 우선 활용, 자산 유동화를 통한 추가 재원 확보도 병행 
   차입금·금융비용 부담을 축소해 재무지표 개선 및 건전성 확보 추진 
   2026 년 본사 추가 차입 계획은 없으며 해외법인은 기존 약정 차입금 일부 추가 인출 예정 
   2027 년은 기존 차입금 차환·운전자금 범위에서 최소한의 자금 조달 계획 
   영업현금 확대·투자 부담 완화에 따른 잉여 재원으로 차입금 감축 기조 지속. 구체적 조달 규모는 미제시

Q4-2. 유럽 IAA 정책 방향에 대응한 중장기 점유율 확보 전략은? 
  A. IAA 는 아직 최종안 미확정이나 수정 권고안상 외국인 직접투자·현지화 요건 강화 예상 
   배터리뿐 아니라 주요 소재의 현지 생산 역량 중요성이 확대될 전망 
   유럽 OEM 의 공급망 다변화로 현지 생산 체계를 갖춘 비중국 업체 소싱 니즈 확대, 경쟁 환경은 우호적 판단 
   폴란드 현지 양산 및 소재·부품 공급망을 이미 구축해 현지 배터리 소싱 수요에 신속 대응 가능 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 에 대한 유럽 고객 관심 증가. 폴란드 감가상각비 감소도 가격 경쟁력 확보에 기여 
   고전압 Mid-Ni 외 LFP·CTP 등 다양한 제품·솔루션으로 신규 수주를 확보해 유럽 점유율 확대 추진

Document 365: How Spain’s parties plan to raise incomes as low earners gain ground

Source PDF: all report pdf/How Spain’s parties plan to raise incomes as low earners gain ground.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Spain's monthly pay rose faster at the bottom than across
much of the middle, though the largest gains were
concentrated near the top. The political parties diﬀer on how
to raise incomes. Some favour a higher minimum wage and
shorter working hours; others emphasise tax cuts and hiring
incentives, or propose deregulation and tighter access to
beneﬁts
Pablo Muylle
Ahead of the election on 29 November, Spain's political parties are oﬀering
diﬀerent ways to increase pay

Between 2023 and 2025, average gross monthly pay rose by 10%
in Spain’s lowest wage decile, compared with around 7% to 8%
across deciles two to six. The three highest deciles recorded gains of
11% to 12%.
Over the longer period from 2019, the bottom decile recorded the
strongest pay growth. Average gross monthly pay rose by about
33%, compared with 23% in the ﬁfth decile and 27% in the top
decile. Spain’s sharp minimum-wage increases are likely to have

### Page 2

supported earnings near the bottom, although these ﬁgures
cannot isolate their eﬀect. Workers move between deciles, while
changes in hours worked, occupation and seniority also aﬀect
average monthly pay.
Wage growth has been strongest at the
bottom and top
Change in average gross monthly wage by overall wage decile, 2023-
2025 (%)
Source: INE, ING Economic Research
Faster growth has not removed the large diﬀerences in pay. In
2025, employees in the lowest overall wage decile earned an

average of €709 per month, compared with €1,970 in the ﬁfth
decile and €5,983 in the 10th.
With Spain voting on 29 November, the election programmes oﬀer
three diﬀerent approaches to raising household incomes. The
Spanish Socialist Workers' Party (PSOE) wants to continue raising
statutory wages, reduce working time and expand social support.
Partido Popular places more weight on tax relief, incentives for
stable employment and refundable family beneﬁts. Vox proposes

broader tax cuts, less labour regulation and tighter eligibility rules
for social assistance.
Low monthly wages also reﬂect limited
working hours

Performance Cookies

### Page 3

Monthly earnings depend on working time as well as wage rates. In
2025, 86.3% of employees in the lowest monthly-wage decile
worked part-time. The share fell to 32.2% in the second decile and
to around 9.5% and 7.5% in the third and fourth deciles,
respectively. Part-time employment was uncommon from the
middle of the distribution onward.
Temporary contracts followed a similar, though less extreme,
pattern. They accounted for 32.7% of employees in the ﬁrst decile

and 18.9% in the second, compared with 3.9% in the top decile.
Employees with a foreign nationality represented more than one-
ﬁfth of the ﬁrst three deciles: 20.9%, 24.5%, and 25.0%,
respectively.
Part-time and temporary work are
concentrated in lower wage deciles
Share of employees in each overall wage decile, 2025 (%)
Source: INE, ING Economic Research
These ﬁgures put the minimum wage debate into perspective. A
higher wage ﬂoor can raise hourly or full-time-equivalent pay, but it

cannot guarantee enough paid hours. Someone working a short or
irregular schedule can remain on a low monthly income even when
the statutory rate rises. Contract stability, predictable schedules
and access to full-time work therefore matter alongside the wage
ﬂoor.

### Page 4

The programmes rely on diﬀerent combinations of wage regulation,
working-time rules, tax reductions, cash beneﬁts and public
services.
PSOE would continue the wage-led
approach
PSOE would continue using the minimum wage as part of a
broader labour market strategy. Since 2018, the monthly rate has
risen from €736 to €1,221, paid 14 times a year. The programme
would place the minimum wage above 60% of the average wage in
law, with future adjustments reﬂecting prices, wages, productivity

and workers’ needs.
Spain has already moved substantially towards a higher wage
ﬂoor. The minimum wage rose from 41.0% of the median full-time
wage in 2018 to 55.3% in 2025, an increase of more than 14
percentage points. France remained above 60%, while Germany
and the Netherlands recorded much smaller increases.
Spain’s minimum wage has moved much
closer to the median wage
Statutory minimum wage as a percentage of the median full-time wage
Source: OECD, ING Economic Research

The chart above uses the median full-time wage, while PSOE’s
proposed legal benchmark refers to the average wage, so the
measures are not directly comparable. Both show that further

### Page 5

increases would start from a much higher relative level than in
2018. Low-paid employees have recorded the strongest wage
growth while employment has continued to rise, but additional
increases may be harder for businesses with low productivity or
narrow margins.
PSOE would also reduce the standard working week to 37.5 hours
without loss of pay and encourage collective bargaining towards 35
hours. Transitional arrangements would support smaller
companies, but the combination of higher wages and shorter

hours would still raise hourly labour costs. Firms would need to
respond through higher productivity, lower margins, higher prices
or changes in employment and hours.
The party also wants employers to justify why positions are not
oﬀered on a full-time basis and would treat contracts covering
more than 80% of a standard schedule as full-time. This targets an
important source of low monthly earnings: insuﬃcient paid hours.
Beyond the labour market, PSOE proposes more support for lower-

income families through a progressive child beneﬁt, a refundable
child tax credit and wider access to childcare and care services.
These measures could reach households that receive little from
conventional tax allowances, but would also create permanent
ﬁscal costs.
Partido Popular would focus more on
disposable income
Partido Popular focuses less on statutory wage targets or shorter
working hours and more on tax cuts, incentives for stable
employment and support for families.

The party proposes lower personal income tax rates for low and
middle-income households, higher personal and family
allowances, and an automatic income tax exemption for anyone
earning the minimum wage. Tax thresholds would again be
adjusted for inﬂation. Workers and self-employed people aged 18
to 35 would receive a four-year tax reduction, beginning with a full
exemption in the ﬁrst year and declining thereafter.
Your privacy is important

Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 6

Tax reductions can increase take-home pay without adding directly
to employers’ wage bills. They may therefore carry less immediate
employment risk than another large increase in the wage ﬂoor.
Their reach is weaker at the bottom, however. Workers whose
earnings already fall below the income-tax threshold gain little
from a conventional tax cut. This includes many people with short
working hours.
Partido Popular partly addresses this through refundable family

support. Existing deductions would be combined into a monthly
payment available even when a household’s tax liability was too
low to use the full deduction. The programme also proposes free
education from birth to age three, more ﬂexible arrangements for
caregivers and a deduction covering 40% of professional care
expenses for an elderly dependent relative.
Employers would pay lower unemployment contributions when
the stability of their workforce exceeded the average in their sector.

New self-employed workers would pay no social security
contributions during their ﬁrst year, with a possible second year for
those earning below the minimum wage.
The distributional eﬀect would depend on the balance between
conventional tax relief, which oﬀers less to people with little taxable
income, and refundable support that can reach poorer households.
Vox would cut taxes and narrow access to
beneﬁts
Vox also relies heavily on lower taxation. It proposes a simpler

personal income tax system, with rates adjusted for the number of
children. Self-employed workers earning below the minimum wage
would pay no social security contributions, with contributions
rising progressively above that threshold.
Vox would repeal the current government’s labour legislation, which
it argues has failed to eliminate insecure employment. The
programme promises stable jobs and higher wages, but provides
little detail on the rules that would replace the existing framework

or how wages would increase.

### Page 7

The party also proposes free education from birth to age three,
childcare support and stronger care services. Access would be more
restrictive, however. Spanish nationals would receive priority for
housing and various social beneﬁts, and the minimum-income
scheme would be limited to them.
Foreign nationals are disproportionately represented in the lower
wage deciles. These restrictions would therefore reduce support for
a group with relatively high exposure to low pay, temporary

employment and housing pressure.
The choice concerns instruments, recipients
and ﬁnancing
Gross wages are only one part of household living standards. Tax
cuts can raise net earnings but oﬀer little to people with low taxable
income, while refundable beneﬁts and public services can reach
those households more directly.
The programmes follow a clear left-right divide. PSOE relies on wage
regulation, shorter hours, employment protection and public
provision. Partido Popular emphasises tax relief, refundable family

support and employer incentives. Vox combines broader tax cuts
and less labour market regulation with tighter access to beneﬁts.
Ahead of the election on 29 November, most polls point to a
parliamentary majority for Partido Popular and Vox. If that
produces a government, policy would probably shift towards tax
relief, employer incentives and greater labour-market ﬂexibility. The
extent of the change would depend on the seat distribution and
Vox’s inﬂuence.

Spain has shown that strong wage growth at the bottom can
coexist with rising employment. Repeating that performance may
be harder from today’s higher wage ﬂoor, especially when many
low-paid employees lack access to suﬃcient working hours or
stable contracts. Higher wage ﬂoors and shorter working weeks
carry risks for low-productivity employers, while conventional tax
cuts often miss the lowest earners. The challenge for the next

Document 366: Huanging On To The Bucking Bull

Source PDF: all report pdf/Huanging On To The Bucking Bull.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Huanging On To The Bucking Bull
Date:    9 hours ago
Huanging On To The Bucking Bull

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Huanging On To The Bucking
Bull
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 24 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Gilley Aguilar / Unsplash
I. Jensen Returns
In the 1984 sci-fi blockbuster, "The Terminator," Arnold Schwarzenegger
famously warned, "I'll be back." It took Jensen Huang just 11 days to make
his comeback. And it certainly was a blockbuster comeback!
Jensen's reinstatement as Nvidia's CEO on Monday triggered a
spectacular reversal of last week's stock market rout. The Nasdaq, S&P

### Page 3

500, and DJIA enjoyed their best day since President Trump announced a
90-day pause on most of his Liberation Day tariffs in April 2025. Nvidia
soared 31.5%, its biggest daily percentage gain in 23 years, adding a
record $950 billion to its market capitalization. Meanwhile, the
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index jumped 14.0%, its fourth-largest daily
gain since 1994. Neoclouds joined the rally, with CoreWeave and Nebius
both gaining more than 20%.
Some of that enthusiasm faded today. The Nasdaq 100 fell 2.0%,

remaining well below its 50-day and 200-day moving averages (charts).
The S&P 500 declined 0.5%, while its equal-weighted counterpart gained
0.5%, reflecting renewed rotation out of technology stocks. The
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index tumbled 7.9%, with Nvidia, AMD, and
Micron falling 5.9%, 8.9%, and 11.0%, respectively. Neoclouds IREN,
CoreWeave, and Nebius tumbled 11.7%, 11.9%, and 10.7%, respectively.

### Page 4

So, what changed? Consider the following:
(1) The Huang Man. On Monday, investors celebrated the reopening of
the AI trade's central bank. Under Jensen's leadership, Nvidia has eased
financial constraints on the AI buildout directly through capital injections
and financial backstops and indirectly through Jensen's votes of
confidence, which facilitate access to capital and lower borrowing costs
across the AI ecosystem.
Today, investors appeared to focus on the political ramifications of his

return. President Trump reportedly facilitated Jensen's reinstatement by
assuring Nvidia's board that he posed no national security threat, citing
the importance of America's technological leadership. However, the
decision has triggered bipartisan pushback, with Democratic Senator
Elizabeth Warren calling it "reckless" and Republican Senator John
Cornyn questioning the national security assurances.
In our view, Jensen's return could intensify the regulatory pressure on the

AI buildout already unleashed by the Democrats' midterm sweep,
emboldening China hawks to pursue tighter semiconductor export controls
and inviting greater congressional scrutiny of the AI ecosystem's business
practices and financing arrangements. So while Jensen's return as
Nvidia's CEO may have eased concerns about financial constraints on the
AI buildout, political constraints could intensify.
Nevertheless, we continue to view financial, political, and physical

constraints as speed bumps for the AI investment boom. They will slow

### Page 5

the buildout but won't brake it, as we expect demand for compute to
continue growing.
(2) Section 301. Reports broke today that the White House and USTR are
preparing to announce a fresh wave of Section 301 tariffs targeting
structural excess manufacturing capacity and state subsidies across 16
major economies—including China, the European Union, Japan, South
Korea, India, Vietnam, and Mexico.
(3) Export controls. Markets are also digesting the Commerce

Department's recent interim rule tightening export licensing requirements
on advanced AI chips to five countries (Singapore, Malaysia, Thailand,
Indonesia, and Brazil) and restricting overseas data centers from renting
substantial computing power to Chinese-headquartered firms. Yesterday
evening, the Commerce Department's Bureau of Industry and Security
added Singapore-based Megaspeed International to the US trade blacklist
for allegedly diverting restricted Nvidia H100 and Blackwell GPUs.

II. US Economy Powers On
While the stock market remains preoccupied with the brouhaha
surrounding the AI trade, the US economy continues to power ahead, and
not merely on the strength of the AI investment boom. Consider the
following:
(1) Consumer Spending. Redbook same-store sales rose 10.3% y/y in
the week ended November 20, well above their 2025 average of 5.8%
(chart). Consumers don't seem to be reining in their spending heading into
the holiday season.

### Page 6

(2) Labor Market. One reason consumer spending should remain strong
is the improving labor market. The Conference Board's November
consumer confidence survey showed that the percentage of consumers
reporting that jobs are plentiful jumped to 30.8%, while the percentage
reporting that jobs are hard to get fell to 18.4%, both their most favorable
readings since early 2025 (chart).
Meanwhile, ADP reported that private-sector employment increased by an

average of 21,000 per week over the four weeks ended November 7, well
above the 16,000 average since 2025 (chart).

### Page 7

(3) Business activity. S&P Global's flash November M-PMI and NM-PMI
eased to 54.5 and 54.7, respectively, but remained comfortably in
expansionary territory (chart). Encouragingly, growth remained broad-
based across both sectors, with hiring activity pointing to another strong
payroll gain in November. Meanwhile, S&P Global's Chris Williamson
noted that the survey's price gauges remain consistent with inflation
running well above the Fed's 2%.
(4) Professor Copper. Then again, the plunge in the copper price in

recent days suggests that the red metal (with a PhD in economics) is
signaling a slower AI buildout, which could slow the economy (chart). The
dollar's recent strength could do the same.

### Page 8

clause.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds. Near-
term event risks and high earnings expectations prompt us to trim equities to a 
Core holding. The US and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain Overweight gold. Prices are likely to grind higher as near-term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead. In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran. These concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly for the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite the current challenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop. The US economic outlook remains well supported, and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA 
Chief Investment Officer, North Asia

Global Market Outlook 4

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels 
associated with flat-to-modestly-negative equity returns  
 Fig. 2 Negative gold-USD correlation has held back

Our quantitative stock-bond model  Gold and USD correlations over the last 15 years

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long-term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and AxJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect them to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers. Our positive view on both markets’ 
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India. Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI-related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification. In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment-grade (IG) 
and high-yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3-7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance. We close our EU bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream, while rotating into alternatives 
helps reduce equity beta exposure. We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub-
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

The Foundation and Foundation+ models are allocations that you can use as the starting point for building a diversified

accessible to most investors, while the Foundation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk. While we remain constructive on the long-term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near-term risk-reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long-term AI growth opportunities. The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI-related beneficiaries. For investors, the 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing 
AI concentration risk through a Total

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk, and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem. 
Non-AI-related factors, including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk. This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50% to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses, although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI-related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversification. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long-term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives, and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We maintain the probability at 30% as AI-driven investment growth continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027, as AI-led expansion continues. The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4%, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2.3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026, but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risks 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees core PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2.4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates at 4.0%, after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
ECB to hike once more in December, then pause. The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5% y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer-term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy, and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year. Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets, which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non-manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD 133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though, with technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first-home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local-government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a quarterly pace until Q2 2027. Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4%, and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five-year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3% in Q1 2027. Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle in H2 2027. Two government-appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

Global Market Outlook 11

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an Underweight view on Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data. With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3-7-year part of the US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.
